[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Design a module named fp_mul_e5f10 that multiplies two floating-point numbers. Format: 16-bit (1 sign + 5 exponent + 10 fraction). Inputs: a and b (16-bit each). Output: y (16-bit). Subnormal numbers must be supported.

Required behaviour, bit-exact: subnormal inputs are used at their true value; results are rounded to nearest, ties to even, including results in the subnormal range; a NaN input and inf x 0 give the canonical quiet NaN 16'h7E00; inf x finite gives inf with the product's sign; an overflowing product gives inf; a zero product gives a signed zero. The testbench enumerates about 18.9 million operand pairs against a reference implementation.

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` in your workspace, a plain readable implementation (unpack, significand multiply, normalise, round, pack) that is correct. Optimize from there.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.

module fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);
  input  [15:0] a;
  input  [15:0] b;
  output [15:0] y;

  localparam BIAS         = 15;
  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN
  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;
  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product

  // 1. unpack
  wire       s_a = a[15];
  wire       s_b = b[15];
  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];
  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];
  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];
  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];

  // 2. classify
  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);
  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);
  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);
  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);
  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);
  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);
  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);
  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);
  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);

  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);
  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;
  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;
  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;

  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)
  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};
  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};
  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;
  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;

  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;

  reg [4:0] lz;
  integer   i;
  always @* begin
    lz = 5'd0;
    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)
      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];
  end

  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;
  genvar k;
  generate
    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below
      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];
    end
  endgenerate

  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector
    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;
    input integer      idx;
    begin
      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;
    end
  endfunction

  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10
  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);
  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)
  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)
  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);
  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];

  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out
  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard
  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even

  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};
  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one
  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];
  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];

  // 6. exponent of a normal result
  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};
  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};
  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};
  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};
  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result
  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number
  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;
  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];

  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once
  integer           total_shift;
  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;
  always @* begin
    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});
    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};
  end
  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];
  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);
  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);
  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);
  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};
  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal
  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];
  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};

  // 8. pack
  wire is_nan      = nan_in;
  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));
  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;
  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);
  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);

  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;
  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;
  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;

  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};
endmodule

`timescale 1ns/10ps
// exhaustive-directed tb (fpmul_run)
// Golden-referenced directed testbench: compares the DUT against the embedded
// golden reference (fp_mul_e5f10_ref, generated from context/starting_point.py)
// over ~18.9M enumerated vectors — no stimuli file. Structure:
//   1. the 3 known subnormal-boundary regression pairs (+ golden self-checks)
//   2. every subnormal/zero a (2^11 sign+mantissa patterns) x 4096 structured b
//   3. 4096 structured a x every subnormal/zero b
//   4. 2^21 LFSR-driven broad pairs
// Structured operands: both signs x all 32 exponents x 64 mantissas
// (8 corners + 56 LFSR-derived). The retired file-driven testbench is kept at
// tb_vectors.sv (+ vectors.dat) for cross-checking; it is not compiled.
module tb;
  logic [15:0] a, b, y, y_ref;
  int errors;
  longint total;

  fp_mul_e5f10 dut (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y));
  fp_mul_e5f10_ref refm (.a(a), .b(b), .y(y_ref));

  function automatic [15:0] lfsr_next(input [15:0] s);
    lfsr_next = (s >> 1) ^ (s[0] ? 16'hB400 : 16'h0000);
  endfunction

  logic [15:0] structured [0:4095];
  localparam logic [9:0] MANT_CORNERS [0:7] =
      '{10'h000, 10'h001, 10'h002, 10'h003, 10'h3FF, 10'h3FE, 10'h2AA, 10'h155};

  task automatic check();
    #1;
    total = total + 1;
    if (y !== y_ref) begin
      if (errors < 10)
        $display("TB_ERROR a=%0d b=%0d expected_y=%0d actual_y=%0d", a, b, y_ref, y);
      errors = errors + 1;
    end
  endtask

  logic [15:0] lfsr, ra, rb;
  logic [9:0] mant;
  int idx;

  initial begin
    errors = 0; total = 0;

    lfsr = 16'hACE1;
    idx = 0;
    for (int s = 0; s < 2; s++)
      for (int e = 0; e < 32; e++)
        for (int m = 0; m < 64; m++) begin
          if (m < 8) mant = MANT_CORNERS[m];
          else begin lfsr = lfsr_next(lfsr); mant = lfsr[9:0]; end
          structured[idx] = {s[0], e[4:0], mant};
          idx = idx + 1;
        end

    a = 16'h0003; b = 16'h6801; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h0E02) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 1"); end
    a = 16'h8003; b = 16'h6479; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h8AB6) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 2"); end
    a = 16'h7521; b = 16'h0003; check();
    if (y_ref !== 16'h1BB2) begin errors++; $display("TB_ERROR golden self-check 3"); end

    for (int sa = 0; sa < 2048; sa++) begin
      a = {sa[10], 5'b00000, sa[9:0]};
      for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin b = structured[i]; check(); end
    end
    for (int i = 0; i < 4096; i++) begin
      a = structured[i];
      for (int sb = 0; sb < 2048; sb++) begin b = {sb[10], 5'b00000, sb[9:0]}; check(); end
    end
    ra = 16'h0001; rb = 16'hBEEF;
    for (int k = 0; k < (1 << 21); k++) begin
      ra = lfsr_next(ra); rb = lfsr_next(lfsr_next(rb));
      a = ra; b = rb; check();
    end

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total, errors);
    if (errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1 sign, 5 exponent, 10 fraction bits) with subnormal support.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  localparam BIAS         = 15;\n  localparam MAX_E        = 31;   // inf / NaN\n  localparam MAX_FINITE_E = 30;\n  localparam PROD_W       = 22;   // 11 x 11 bit significand product\n\n  // 1. unpack\n  wire       s_a = a[15];\n  wire       s_b = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] e_a = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] e_b = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] f_a = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] f_b = b[9:0];\n\n  // 2. classify\n  wire e_a_zero = (e_a == 5'd0);\n  wire e_b_zero = (e_b == 5'd0);\n  wire e_a_ones = (e_a == MAX_E);\n  wire e_b_ones = (e_b == MAX_E);\n  wire zero_a   = e_a_zero & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire zero_b   = e_b_zero & (f_b == 10'd0);\n  wire nan_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a != 10'd0);\n  wire nan_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b != 10'd0);\n  wire inf_a    = e_a_ones & (f_a == 10'd0);\n  wire inf_b    = e_b_ones & (f_b == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in  = nan_a | nan_b | (inf_a & zero_b) | (zero_a & inf_b);\n  wire inf_in  = inf_a | inf_b;\n  wire zero_in = zero_a | zero_b;\n  wire s_y     = s_a ^ s_b;\n\n  // 3. effective significands and exponents (subnormal: no hidden one, exponent 1)\n  wire [10:0] m_a    = {~e_a_zero, f_a};\n  wire [10:0] m_b    = {~e_b_zero, f_b};\n  wire [4:0]  ea_eff = e_a_zero ? 5'd1 : e_a;\n  wire [4:0]  eb_eff = e_b_zero ? 5'd1 : e_b;\n\n  // 4. significand product, its leading-zero count (0 for a zero product), below[k] = |prod[k:0]\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] prod = m_a * m_b;\n\n  reg [4:0] lz;\n  integer   i;\n  always @* begin\n    lz = 5'd0;\n    for (i = 0; i < PROD_W; i = i + 1)\n      if (prod[i]) lz = 5'd21 - i[4:0];\n  end\n\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] below;\n  genvar k;\n  generate\n    for (k = 0; k < PROD_W; k = k + 1) begin : g_below\n      assign below[k] = |prod[k:0];\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  function bit_at;   // v[idx], 0 outside the vector\n    input [PROD_W-1:0] v;\n    input integer      idx;\n    begin\n      bit_at = (idx >= 0 && idx < PROD_W) ? v[idx] : 1'b0;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  // 5. normalise and round a normal result: leading one onto bit 10\n  wire              need_right = (lz <= 5'd11);\n  wire [4:0]        sr         = 5'd11 - lz;   // right shift (wide product)\n  wire [4:0]        sl         = lz - 5'd11;   // left shift (narrow product)\n  wire [PROD_W-1:0] shifted    = need_right ? (prod >> sr) : (prod << sl);\n  wire [10:0]       mant_pre   = shifted[10:0];\n\n  wire guard    = need_right ? bit_at(prod,  sr - 1) : 1'b0;   // first bit shifted out\n  wire sticky   = need_right ? bit_at(below, sr - 2) : 1'b0;   // any bit below the guard\n  wire round_up = guard & (sticky | mant_pre[0]);              // round to nearest, ties to even\n\n  wire [11:0] mant_round = {1'b0, mant_pre} + {11'd0, round_up};\n  wire        carry      = mant_round[11];   // rounded up to a new leading one\n  wire [10:0] mant_post  = carry ? mant_round[11:1] : mant_round[10:0];\n  wire [9:0]  frac_norm  = mant_post[9:0];\n\n  // 6. exponent of a normal result\n  wire [7:0] exp_sum     = {3'd0, ea_eff} + {3'd0, eb_eff};\n  wire [7:0] lhs         = exp_sum + 8'd1 + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire [7:0] limit_under = BIAS + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire [7:0] limit_over  = BIAS + MAX_FINITE_E + {3'd0, lz};\n  wire       underflow   = (lhs <= limit_under);   // subnormal (or zero) result\n  wire       overflow    = (lhs >  limit_over);    // past the largest finite number\n  wire [7:0] e_norm      = lhs - limit_under;\n  wire [4:0] exp_norm    = e_norm[4:0];\n\n  // 7. subnormal result: shift the raw product onto the subnormal grid, round once\n  integer           total_shift;\n  reg [PROD_W-1:0]  sub_shifted;\n  always @* begin\n    total_shift = (BIAS + 10 + 1) - $signed({1'b0, exp_sum});\n    sub_shifted = (total_shift >= 0 && total_shift < PROD_W) ? (prod >> total_shift) : {PROD_W{1'b0}};\n  end\n  wire [9:0]  frac_trunc = sub_shifted[9:0];\n  wire        guard_s    = bit_at(prod,  total_shift - 1);\n  wire        sticky_s   = bit_at(below, total_shift - 2);\n  wire        round_s    = guard_s & (sticky_s | frac_trunc[0]);\n  wire [10:0] frac_sum   = {1'b0, frac_trunc} + {10'd0, round_s};\n  wire        carry_s    = frac_sum[10];   // rounded up to the smallest normal\n  wire [9:0]  frac_sub   = frac_sum[9:0];\n  wire [4:0]  exp_sub    = {4'd0, carry_s};\n\n  // 8. pack\n  wire is_nan      = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf      = ~is_nan & (inf_in | (overflow & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub      = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & underflow;\n  wire sub_is_zero = is_sub & (exp_sub == 5'd0) & (frac_sub == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero     = ~is_nan & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_is_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] exp_y  = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 : is_zero ? 5'd0 : is_sub ? exp_sub : exp_norm;\n  wire [9:0] frac_y = is_nan ? 10'h200 : (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 : is_sub ? frac_sub : frac_norm;\n  wire       sign_y = is_nan ? 1'b0 : s_y;\n\n  assign y = {sign_y, exp_y, frac_y};\nendmodule\n", filename='design.v')

The baseline is ~145k. Now let me implement a restructured design: pre-normalize the (at most one) subnormal input so the product needs only a 1-bit normalize, and derive the subnormal-result path from the normalized product with a small shifter.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Structure: normalise the (at most one) subnormal significand before the\n// multiply so the 11x11 product always lands in [2^20,2^22) -> 1-bit normalise.\n// The subnormal result is derived from the normalised product with a small\n// (10-bit) shifter driven by the result exponent.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  function [2:0] clz5;\n    input [4:0] x;\n    begin\n      if      (x[4]) clz5 = 3'd0;\n      else if (x[3]) clz5 = 3'd1;\n      else if (x[2]) clz5 = 3'd2;\n      else if (x[1]) clz5 = 3'd3;\n      else           clz5 = 3'd4;\n    end\n  endfunction\n\n  wire       sa = a[15];\n  wire       sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0] ea = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0] eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0] fa = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0);\n  wire eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eaX = (ea == 5'd31);\n  wire ebX = (eb == 5'd31);\n  wire za  = ea0 & (fa == 10'd0);\n  wire zb  = eb0 & (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire na  = eaX & (fa != 10'd0);\n  wire nb  = ebX & (fb != 10'd0);\n  wire ia  = eaX & (fa == 10'd0);\n  wire ib  = ebX & (fb == 10'd0);\n\n  wire nan_in   = na | nb | (ia & zb) | (za & ib);\n  wire inf_in   = ia | ib;\n  wire zero_in  = za | zb;\n  wire both_sub = ea0 & eb0;      // product is 0 or underflows to 0\n  wire sgn      = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- normalise the (at most one) subnormal significand ----\n  wire [9:0] fsub = ea0 ? fa : fb;\n  wire [4:0] fh = fsub[9:5];\n  wire [4:0] fl = fsub[4:0];\n  wire       hnz = |fh;\n  wire [2:0] czh = clz5(fh);\n  wire [2:0] czl = clz5(fl);\n  wire [3:0] clz = hnz ? {1'b0, czh} : (4'd5 + {1'b0, czl});\n  wire [9:0] gn  = hnz ? {fh << czh, fl} : {fl << czl, 5'b0};\n\n  wire [10:0] ma = ea0 ? {gn, 1'b0} : {1'b1, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = eb0 ? {gn, 1'b0} : {1'b1, fb};\n  wire [4:0]  lzab = (ea0 ? (5'd1 + {1'b0, clz}) : 5'd0)\n                   + (eb0 ? (5'd1 + {1'b0, clz}) : 5'd0);\n\n  // ---- significand product, leading one forced to bit 21 ----\n  wire [21:0] pp  = ma * mb;\n  wire        n1  = pp[21];\n  wire [21:0] nrm = n1 ? pp : {pp[20:0], 1'b0};\n\n  // ---- round the normal result ----\n  wire [10:0] mpre  = nrm[21:11];\n  wire        grdn  = nrm[10];\n  wire        stkn  = |nrm[9:0];\n  wire        rndn  = grdn & (stkn | mpre[0]);\n  wire [11:0] msum  = {1'b0, mpre} + {11'd0, rndn};\n  wire        carry = msum[11];\n  wire [9:0]  fracn = msum[9:0];\n\n  // ---- result exponent ----\n  wire [6:0] exps = {2'd0, ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea} + {2'd0, eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb};\n  wire [7:0] enc  = {1'b0, exps} - 8'd15 - {3'd0, lzab} + {7'd0, n1};\n  wire [7:0] eE   = enc + {7'd0, carry};\n  wire       sub_res = eE[7] | (eE == 8'd0);\n  wire       ovr     = ~eE[7] & (eE > 8'd30);\n\n  // ---- subnormal result path ----\n  wire [7:0] negc = (~enc) + 8'd1;\n  wire [4:0] dd   = enc[7] ? negc[4:0] : 5'd0;\n  wire [9:0]  v   = nrm[21:12];\n  wire [11:0] low = nrm[11:0];\n  wire [9:0]  f10   = v >> dd;\n  wire [9:0]  vsub  = v >> (dd - 5'd1);\n  wire [9:0]  vleft = v << (5'd11 - dd);\n  wire       grds = (dd == 5'd0) ? low[11] : vsub[0];\n  wire       stks = (dd == 5'd0) ? |low[10:0] : (|low | |vleft);\n  wire       rnds = grds & (stks | f10[0]);\n  wire [10:0] fsum = {1'b0, f10} + {10'd0, rnds};\n  wire        carry_s = fsum[10];\n  wire [9:0]  fracs = fsum[9:0];\n\n  // ---- pack ----\n  wire is_nan   = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf   = ~nan_in & (inf_in | (ovr & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub   = ~nan_in & ~is_inf & sub_res;\n  wire sub_zero = is_sub & ~carry_s & (fracs == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero  = ~nan_in & ~is_inf & (zero_in | both_sub | sub_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] ey = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 :\n                  is_zero ? 5'd0 :\n                  is_sub  ? {4'd0, carry_s} : eE[4:0];\n  wire [9:0] fy = is_nan ? 10'h200 :\n                  (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 :\n                  is_sub ? fracs : fracn;\n  wire sy = is_nan ? 1'b0 : sgn;\n\n  assign y = {sy, ey, fy};\nendmodule\n", filename='designA.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Now let me build an improved variant: keep the pre-normalized multiply (so the product is already normalized), but make the subnormal-result path's shift amount depend only on the *inputs* (not on a product bit), which was designA's critical path.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Structure: pre-normalise the (at most one) subnormal significand so the\n// 11x11 product is already normalised (leading 1 at bit 21 or 20).\n// The subnormal-result path shifts the product with an INPUT-ONLY shift amount\n// s = 15 - (ea+eb) + lz, so no product bit ever feeds a shifter select.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        sa = a[15];\n  wire        sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0);\n  wire eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eaX = (ea == 5'd31);\n  wire ebX = (eb == 5'd31);\n  wire faz = (fa == 10'd0);\n  wire fbz = (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire za  = ea0 & faz;\n  wire zb  = eb0 & fbz;\n  wire na  = eaX & ~faz;\n  wire nb  = ebX & ~fbz;\n  wire ia  = eaX & faz;\n  wire ib  = ebX & fbz;\n\n  wire nan_in  = na | nb | (ia & zb) | (za & ib);\n  wire inf_in  = ia | ib;\n  wire zero_in = za | zb;\n  wire sgn     = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- normalise the (at most one) subnormal significand ----\n  wire [9:0] fsub = ea0 ? fa : fb;\n  wire [3:0] clz =\n      fsub[9]   ? 4'd0 :\n      fsub[8]   ? 4'd1 :\n      fsub[7]   ? 4'd2 :\n      fsub[6]   ? 4'd3 :\n      fsub[5]   ? 4'd4 :\n      fsub[4]   ? 4'd5 :\n      fsub[3]   ? 4'd6 :\n      fsub[2]   ? 4'd7 :\n      fsub[1]   ? 4'd8 : 4'd9;\n\n  wire [9:0]  gn  = fsub << clz;                  // msb at bit 9\n  wire [4:0]  lza = 5'd1 + {1'b0, clz};\n  wire [4:0]  lz  = (ea0 ? lza : 5'd0) + (eb0 ? lza : 5'd0);\n\n  wire [10:0] ma = ea0 ? {gn, 1'b0} : {1'b1, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = eb0 ? {gn, 1'b0} : {1'b1, fb};\n\n  wire [21:0] pp = ma * mb;                       // = raw product << lz\n\n  // ---- exponent sums (input only) ----\n  wire [5:0] exps = {1'b0, ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea} + {1'b0, eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb};\n\n  // ---- normal result path ----\n  wire        n1    = pp[21];\n  wire [22:0] nrm   = n1 ? {1'b0, pp} : {pp, 1'b0};\n  wire [10:0] mpre  = nrm[21:11];\n  wire        grdn  = nrm[10];\n  wire        stkn  = |nrm[9:0];\n  wire        rndn  = grdn & (stkn | mpre[0]);\n  wire [10:0] minc  = {1'b0, mpre[9:0]} + {10'd0, rndn};\n  wire        carry = minc[10];\n  wire [9:0]  fracn = minc[9:0];\n\n  wire [6:0]  enc   = {1'b0, exps} - 7'd15 - {2'b00, lz} + {6'd0, n1};\n  wire [6:0]  eE    = enc + {6'd0, carry};\n  wire        sub_res = eE[6] | (eE == 7'd0);\n  wire        ovr     = ~eE[6] & (eE > 7'd30);\n\n  // ---- subnormal result path: shift amount from inputs only ----\n  // s = 15 - exps + lz   (subnormal result iff enc <= 0, then s >= 0)\n  wire [6:0] sfull = 7'd15 + {2'b00, lz} - {1'b0, exps};\n  wire       sbig  = (sfull > 7'd11);\n  wire [3:0] sh    = sfull[3:0];\n\n  wire [12:0] v13  = pp[21:9];                    // v13[i] = pp[i+9]\n  wire [12:0] w13  = v13 >> sh;                   // 4-stage barrel\n  wire [9:0]  f10  = sbig ? 5'd31 :\n                  is_zero ? 5'd0 :\n                  is_sub  ? {4'd0, carry_s} : eE[4:0];\n  wire [9:0] fy = is_nan ? 10'h200 :\n                  (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 :\n                  is_sub ? [ASSISTANT]
Now the key insight: Yosys maps `*` to a slow shift-add array (~700ps). Let me hand-code a Wallace tree + Kogge-Stone prefix adder for the 11Ã11 multiply, keeping the rest of designC.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Hand-built Wallace tree + Kogge-Stone prefix adder for the 11x11 product.\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        sa = a[15];\n  wire        sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0);\n  wire eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eaX = (ea == 5'd31);\n  wire ebX = (eb == 5'd31);\n  wire faz = (fa == 10'd0);\n  wire fbz = (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire za  = ea0 & faz;\n  wire zb  = eb0 & fbz;\n  wire na  = eaX & ~faz;\n  wire nb  = ebX & ~fbz;\n  wire ia  = eaX & faz;\n  wire ib  = ebX & fbz;\n\n  wire nan_in  = na | nb | (ia & zb) | (za & ib);\n  wire inf_in  = ia | ib;\n  wire zero_in = za | zb;\n  wire sgn     = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- normalise the (at most one) subnormal significand ----\n  wire [9:0] fsub = ea0 ? fa : fb;\n  wire [3:0] clz =\n      fsub[9]   ? 4'd0 :\n      fsub[8]   ? 4'd1 :\n      fsub[7]   ? 4'd2 :\n      fsub[6]   ? 4'd3 :\n      fsub[5]   ? 4'd4 :\n      fsub[4] lza : 5'd0) + (eb0 ? lza : 5'd0);\n\n  wire [10:0] ma = ea0 ? {gn, 1'b0} : {1'b1, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = eb0 ? {gn, 1'b0} : {1'b1, fb};\n\n  // ---- Wallace tree: 11 partial products -> 2 rows << 3)  & {22{mb[3]}};\n  wire [21:0] p4  = ({11'd0, ma} << 4)  & {22{mb[4]}};\n  wire [21:0] p5  = ({11'd0, ma} << 5)  & {22{mb[5]}};\n  wire [21:0] p6  = ({11'd0, ma} << 6)  & {22{mb[6]}};\n  wire [21:0] p7  = ({11'd0, ma} << 7)  & {22{mb[7]}};\n  wire [21:0] p8  = ({11'd0, ma} << 8)  & {22{mb[8]}};\n  wire [21:0] p9  = ({11'd0, ma} << 9)  & {22{mb[9]}};\n  wire [21:0] p10 = ({11'd0, ma} << 10) & {22{mb[10]}};\n\n  wire [21:0] s1 = p0 ^ p1 ^ p2;\n  wire [21:0] c1 = ((p0 & p1) | (p2 & (p0 ^ p1))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s2 = p3 ^ p4 ^ p5;\n  wire [21:0] c2 = ((p3 & p4) | (p5 & (p3 ^ p4))) << = S + C ----\n  wire [21:0] Pp [0:5];\n  wire [21:0] Gg [0:5];\n  assign Pp[0] = S ^ C;\n  assign Gg[0] = S & C;\n  genvar l, i;\n  generate\n    for (l = 1; l <= 5; l = l + 1) begin : gl\n      for (i = 0; i < 22; i = i + 1) begin : gb\n        if (i >= (1 << (l - 1))) begin : black\n          assign Pp[l][i] = Pp[l-1][i] & Pp[l-1][i - (1 << (l - 1))];\n          assign Gg[l][i] = Gg[l-1][i] | [ASSISTANT]
Now design F: remove the pre-normalization shifter (multiplier uses raw significands, starting immediately), with the lz-shift moved after the multiplier (select is input-only), plus select-based rounding (increment computed in parallel).
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Raw-significand multiply (starts immediately from the inputs); the\n// normalisation shift (lz) and the subnormal alignment shift are both\n// input-only-select barrel shifts placed after the multiplier.\n// Rounding uses a parallel increment + select (no serial adder).\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        sa = a[15];\n  wire        sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0);\n  wire eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eaX = (ea == 5'd31);\n  wire ebX = (eb == 5'd31);\n  wire faz = (fa == 10'd0);\n  wire fbz = (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire za  = ea0 & faz;\n  wire zb  = eb0 & fbz;\n  wire na  = eaX & ~faz;\n  wire nb  = ebX & ~fbz;\n  wire ia  = eaX & faz;\n  wire ib  = ebX & fbz;\n\n  wire nan_in  = na | nb | (ia & zb) | (za & ib);\n  wire inf_in  = ia | ib;\n  wire zero_in = za | zb;\n  wire sgn     = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- leading zero counts of the fractions (parallel with the multiplier) ----\n  wire [3:0] clza = fa[9] ? 4'd0 : fa[8] ? 4'd1 : fa[7] ? 4'd2 : fa[6] ? 4'd3 :\n                    fa[5] ? 4'd4 : fa[4] ? 4'd5 : fa[3] ? 4'd6 : fa[2] ? 4'd7 :\n                    fa[1] ? 4'd8 : 4'd9;\n  wire [3:0] clzb = fb[9] ? 4'd0 : fb[8] ? 4'd1 : fb[7] ? 4'd2 : fb[6] ? 4'd3 :\n                    fb[5] ? 4'd4 : fb[4] ? 4'd5 : fb[3] ? 4'd6 : fb[2] ? 4'd7 :\n                    fb[1] ? 4'd8 : 4'd9;\n\n  wire [4:0] lz = (ea0 ? (5'd1 + {1'b0, clza}) : 5'd0)\n                + (eb0 ? (5'd1 + {1'b0, clzb}) : 5'd0);\n\n  // ---- raw significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- Wallace tree: 11 partial products -> 2 rows ----\n  wire [21:0] p0  = {11'd0, ma} & {22{mb[0]}};\n  wire [21:0] p1  = ({11'd0, ma} << 1)  & {22{mb[1]}};\n  wire [21:0] p2  = ({11'd0, ma} << 2)  & {22{mb[2]}};\n  wire [21:0] p3  = ({11'd0, ma} << 3)  & {22{mb[3]}};\n  wire [21:0] p4  = ({11'd0, ma} << 4)  & {22{mb[4]}};\n  wire [21:0] p5  = ({11'd0, ma} << 5)  & {22{mb[5]}};\n  wire [21:0] p6  = ({11'd0, ma} << 6)  & {22{mb[6]}};\n  wire [21:0] p7  = ({11'd0, ma} << 7)  & {22{mb[7]}};\n  wire [21:0] p8  = ({11'd0, ma} << 8)  & {22{mb[8]}};\n  wire [21:0] p9  = ({11'd0, ma} << 9)  & {22{mb[9]}};\n  wire [21:0] p10 = ({11'd0, ma} << 10) & {22{mb[10]}};\n\n  wire [21:0] s1 = p0 ^ p1 ^ p2;\n  wire [21:0] c1 = ((p0 & p1) | (p2 & (p0 ^ p1))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s2 = p3 ^ p4 ^ p5;\n  wire [21:0] c2 = ((p3 & p4) | (p5 & (p3 ^ p4))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s3 = p6 ^ p7 ^ p8;\n  wire [21:0] c3 = ((p6 & p7) | (p8 & (p6 ^ p7))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s4 = s1 ^ c1 ^ s2;\n  wire [21:0] c4 = ((s1 & c1) | (s2 & (s1 ^ c1))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s5 = s3 ^ c3 ^ p9;\n  wire [21:0] c5 = ((s3 & c3) | (p9 & (s3 ^ c3))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s6 = s4 ^ c4 ^ s5;\n  wire [21:0] c6 = ((s4 & c4) | (s5 & (s4 ^ c4))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s7 = c5 ^ c2 ^ p10;\n  wire [21:0] c7 = ((c5 & c2) | (p10 & (c5 ^ c2))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s8 = s6 ^ c6 ^ s7;\n  wire [21:0] c8 = ((s6 & c6) | (s7 & (s6 ^ c6))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] S = s8 ^ c8 ^ c7;\n  wire [21:0] C = ((s8 & c8) | (c7 & (s8 ^ c8))) << 1;\n\n  // ---- Kogge-Stone prefix adder: T = S + C ----\n  wire [21:0] Pp [0:5];\n  wire [21:0] Gg [0:5];\n  assign Pp[0] = S ^ C;\n  assign Gg[0] = S & C;\n  genvar l, i;\n  generate\n    for (l = 1; l <= 5; l = l + 1) begin : gl\n      for (i = 0; i < 22; i = i + 1) begin : gb\n        if (i >= (1 << (l - 1))) begin : black\n          assign Pp[l][i] = Pp[l-1][i] & Pp[l-1][i - (1 << (l - 1))];\n          assign Gg[l][i] = Gg[l-1][i] | (Pp[l-1][i] & Gg[l-1][i - (1 << (l - 1))]);\n        end else begin : pass\n          assign Pp[l][i] = Pp[l-1][i];\n          assign Gg[l][i] = Gg[l-1][i];\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  wire [21:0] T;\n  assign T[0]    = Pp[0][0];\n  assign T[21:1] = Pp[0][21:1] ^ Gg[5][20:0];\n\n  // ---- exponent sums (input only) ----\n  wire [5:0] exps = {1'b0, ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea} + {1'b0, eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb};\n  wire [6:0] P    = {1'b0, exps} - 7'd15 - {2'b00, lz};\n\n  // ---- normal result path: normalise the raw product by lz ----\n  wire [21:0] pp   = T << lz;\n  wire        n1   = pp[21];\n  wire [21:0] nrm  = n1 ? pp : {pp[20:0], 1'b0};\n  wire [10:0] mpre = nrm[21:11];\n  wire        grdn = nrm[10];\n  wire        stkn = |nrm[9:0];\n  wire        rndn = grdn & (stkn | mpre[0]);\n  wire [10:0] incn = {1'b0, mpre[9:0]} + 11'd1;\n  wire [9:0]  fracn = rndn ? incn[9:0] : mpre[9:0];\n  wire        carry = rndn & incn[10];\n\n  wire [6:0]  eE      = P + {6'd0, n1} + {6'd0, carry};\n  wire        sub_res = eE[6] | (eE == 7'd0);\n  wire        ovr     = ~eE[6] & (eE > 7'd30);\n\n  // ---- subnormal result path: align the raw product to the subnormal grid ----\n  wire [4:0]  h5   = 5'd25 - exps[4:0];\n  wire [10:0] w    = T >> h5;\n  wire [9:0]  f10  = w[10:1];\n  wire        grds = w[0];\n  wire [5:0]  sl   = 6'd23 - {1'b0, h5};\n  wire [22:0] st   = {1'b0, T} << sl;\n  wire        stks = |st;\n  wire        rnds = grds & (stks | f10[0]);\n  wire [10:0] incs = {1'b0, f10} + 11'd1;\n  wire [9:0]  fracs = rnds ? incs[9:0] : f10;\n  wire        carry_s = rnds & incs[10];\n\n  // ---- pack ----\n  wire is_nan   = nan_in;\n  wire is_inf   = ~nan_in & (inf_in | (ovr & ~zero_in));\n  wire is_sub   = ~nan_in & ~is_inf & sub_res;\n  wire sub_zero = is_sub & ~carry_s & (fracs == 10'd0);\n  wire is_zero  = ~nan_in & ~is_inf & (zero_in | sub_zero);\n\n  wire [4:0] ey = (is_nan | is_inf) ? 5'd31 :\n                  is_zero ? 5'd0 :\n                  is_sub  ? {4'd0, carry_s} : eE[4:0];\n  wire [9:0] fy = is_nan ? 10'h200 :\n                  (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 :\n                  is_sub ? fracs : fracn;\n  wire sy = is_nan ? 1'b0 : sgn;\n\n  assign y = {sy, ey, fy};\nendmodule\n", filename='designF.v')

Now the key step: restrict the lz-range for both the normalize and subnormal shifts. Since only a subnormal input makes lz nonzero, and lz â¤ 10 (one operand subnormal), the shifts can be 4-bit. With an 11-bit lz shift (bit 10 handled by mux instead of the barrel).
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Raw-significand multiply (starts immediately from the inputs); the\n// normalisation shift (lz, 4-bit range) and the subnormal alignment shift\n// are input-only-select barrel shifts placed after the multiplier.\n// Rounding uses a parallel increment + select (no serial adder).\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        sa = a[15];\n  wire        sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0);\n  wire eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eaX = (ea == 5'd31);\n  wire ebX = (eb == 5'd31);\n  wire faz = (fa == 10'd0);\n  wire fbz = (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire za  = ea0 & faz;\n  wire zb  = eb0 & fbz;\n  wire na  = eaX & ~faz;\n  wire nb  = ebX & ~fbz;\n  wire ia  = eaX & faz;\n  wire ib  = ebX & fbz;\n\n  wire nan_in  = na | nb | (ia & zb) | (za & ib);\n  wire inf_in  = ia | ib;\n  wire zero_in = za | zb;\n  wire sgn     = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- leading zero counts of the fractions (parallel with the multiplier) ----\n  wire [3:0] clza = fa[9] ? 4'd0 : fa[8] ? 4'd1 : fa[7] ? 4'd2 : fa[6] ? 4'd3 :\n                    fa[5] ? 4'd4 : fa[4] ? 4'd5 : fa[3] ? 4'd6 : fa[2] ? 4'd7 :\n                    fa[1] ? 4'd8 : 4'd9;\n  wire [3:0] clzb = fb[9] ? 4'd0 : fb[8] ? 4'd1 : fb[7] ? 4'd2 : fb[6] ? 4'd3 :\n                    fb[5] ? 4'd4 : fb[4] ? 4'd5 : fb[3] ? 4'd6 : fb[2] ? 4'd7 :\n                    fb[1] ? 4'd8 : 4'd9;\n\n  wire [4:0] lz = (ea0 ? (5'd1 + {1'b0, clzb}) : 5'd0);   // 0..10\n\n  // ---- raw significands ----\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  // ---- Wallace tree: 11 partial products -> 2 rows ----\n  wire [21:0] p0  = {11'd0, ma} & {22{mb[0]}};\n  wire [21:0] p1  = ({11'd0, ma} << 1)  & {22{mb[1]}};\n  wire [21:0] p2  = ({11'd0, ma} << 2)  & {22{mb[2]}};\n  wire [21:0] p3  = ({11'd0, ma} << 3)  & {22{mb[3]}};\n  wire [21:0] p4  = ({11'd0, ma} << 4)  & {22{mb[4]}};\n  wire [21:0] p5  = ({11'd0, ma} << 5)  & {22{mb[5]}};\n  wire [21:0] p6  = ({11'd0, ma} << 6)  & {22{mb[6]}};\n  wire [21:0] p7  = ({11'd0, ma} << 7)  & {22{mb[7]}};\n  wire [21:0] p8  = ({11'd0, ma} << 8)  & {22{mb[8]}};\n  wire [21:0] p9  = ({11'd0, ma} << 9)  & {22{mb[9]}};\n  wire [21:0] p10 = ({11'd0, ma} << 10) & {22{mb[10]}};\n\n  wire [21:0] s1 = p0 ^ p1 ^ p2;\n  wire [21:0] c1 = ((p0 & p1) | (p2 & (p0 ^ p1))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s2 = p3 ^ p4 ^ p5;\n  wire [21:0] c2 = ((p3 & p4) | (p5 & (p3 ^ p4))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s3 = p6 ^ p7 ^ p8;\n  wire [21:0] c3 = ((p6 & p7) | (p8 & (p6 ^ p7))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s4 = s1 ^ c1 ^ s2;\n  wire [21:0] c4 = ((s1 & c1) | (s2 & (s1 ^ c1))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s5 = s3 ^ c3 ^ p9;\n  wire [21:0] c5 = ((s3 & c3) | (p9 & (s3 ^ c3))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s6 = s4 ^ c4 ^ s5;\n  wire [21:0] c6 = ((s4 & c4) | (s5 & (s4 ^ c4))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s7 = c5 ^ c2 ^ p10;\n  wire [21:0] c7 = ((c5 & c2) | (p10 & (c5 ^ c2))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] s8 = s6 ^ c6 ^ s7;\n  wire [21:0] c8 = ((s6 & c6) | (s7 & (s6 ^ c6))) << 1;\n  wire [21:0] S = s8 ^ c8 ^ c7;\n  wire [21:0] C = ((s8 & c8) | (c7 & (s8 ^ c8))) << 1;\n\n  // ---- Kogge-Stone prefix adder: T = S + C ----\n  wire [21:0] Pp [0:5];\n  wire [21:0] Gg [0:5];\n  assign Pp[0] = S ^ C;\n  assign Gg[0] = S & C;\n  genvar l, i;\n  generate\n    for (l = 1; lz[3:0] via barrel\n  wire        lz10 = (lz == 5'd10);\n  wire [21:0] psh  = T << lz[3:0];\n  wire [21:0] pp   = lz10 ? [ASSISTANT]
Now the big structural idea: **unify** the normal and subnormal paths. Both fraction windows are right-shifts of the product T: normal = T[s+10:s] with s = 10-lz+n1, sub = T[s+10:s] with s = 26-exps. So one sticky-accumulating right barrel (select = input-only + the early n1 bit) serves both, with one rounding incrementer.
  -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Unified data path: the result fraction is always the 11-bit window\n// T[s+10 : s] of the raw product, right-shifted by d = s-1 so that the\n// guard lands in bit 0.  The barrel select is input-only plus n1 (=T[21],\n// available early), so no product bit drives a shifter select.\n//   normal case : s = 10 - lz + n1     (lz = subnormal normalisation shift)\n//   subnormal   : s = 26 - exps        (subnormal grid 2^-24)\n// Both cases round with one trail-XOR incrementer; the exponent is built\n// with prefix-AND carry-in increments (no adders on the late path).\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        sa = a[15];\n  wire        sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0);\n  wire eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eaX = (ea == 5'd31);\n  wire ebX = (eb == 5'd31);\n  wire faz = (fa == 10'd0);\n  wire fbz = (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire za  = ea0 & faz;\n  wire zb  = eb0 & fbz;\n  wire na  = eaX & ~faz;\n  wire nb  = ebX & ~fbz;\n  wire ia  = eaX & faz;\n  wire ib  = ebX & fbz;\n\n  wire nan_in  = na | nb | (ia & zb) | (za & ib);\n  wire inf_in  = ia | ib;\n  wire zero_in = za | zb;\n  wire sgn     = sa ^ sb;\n\n  // ---- leading zero counts of the fractions (parallel with the multiplier) ----\n  wire [3:0] clza = fa[9] ? 4'd0 : fa[8] ? 4'd1 : fa[7] ? 4'd2 : fa[6] ? 4'd3 :\n                    fa[5] ? 4'd4 : fa[4] ? 4'd5 : fa[3] ? 4'd6 : fa[2] ? 4'd7 :\n                    fa[1] ? 4'd8 : 4'd9;\n  wire [3:0] clzb = fb[9] ? 4'd0 : fb[8] ? 4'd1 : fb[7] ? 4'd2 : fb[6] ? 4'd3 :\n                    fb[5] ? 4'd4 : fb[4] ? 4'd5 : fb[3] ? 4'd6 : fb[2] ? 4'd7 :\n                    fb[1] ? 4'd8 : 4'd9;\n\n  wire [4:0] lz = (ea0 ? (5'd1 + {1'b0, clza}) : 5'd0)\n                + (eb0 ? l <= 5; l = l + 1) begin : gl\n      for (i = 0; i < 22; i = i + 1) begin : gb\n        if ((i % (1 << l)) >= (1 << (l - 1))) begin : black\n          localparam integer j = (i & ~((1 << l) - 1)) + (1 << (l - 1)) - 1;\n          assign Pp[l][i] = Pp[l-1][i] & Pp[l-1][j];\n          assign Gg[l][i] = Gg[l-1][i] | (Pp[l-1][i] & Gg[l-1][j]);\n        end else begin : pass\n          assign Pp[l][i] = Pp[l-1][i];\n          assign Gg[l][i] = Gg[l-1][i];\n        end\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  wire [21:0] T;\n  assign T[0]    = Pp[0][0];\n  assign T[21:1] = Pp[0][21:1] ^ Gg[5][20:0];\n  wire n1 = T[21];\n\n  // ---- exponent arithmetic (input only) ----\n  wire [5:0] exps = {1'b0, ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea} + {1'b0, eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb};\n  wire [6:0] P    = {1'b0, exps} - 7'd15 - {2'b00, lz};\n\n  // prefix-AND of P (early): trP[i] = &P[i-1:0]\n  wire [6:0] q1, q2, q3;\n  generate\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : pq0\n      assign q1[i] = (i == 0) ? 1'b1 : P[i-1];\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : pq1\n      if ((i % 2) >= 1) begin : b1\n        localparam integer j1 = (i & ~1);\n        assign q2[i] = q1[i] & q1[j1];\n      end else begin : p1\n        assign q2[i] = q1[i];\n      end\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : pq2\n      if ((i % 4) >= 2) begin : b2\n        localparam integer j2 = (i & ~3) + 1;\n        assign q3[i] = q2[i] & q2[j2];\n      end else begin : p2\n        assign q3[i] = q2[i];\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire [6:0] trP;\n  generate\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : pq3\n      if ((i % 8) >= 4) begin : b3\n        localparam integer j3 = (i & ~7) + 3;\n        assign trP[i] = q3[i] & q3[j3];\n      end else begin : p3\n        assign trP[i] = q3[i];\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // eE0 = P + n1  (biased exponent field before rounding)\n  wire [6:0] eE0 = P ^ (trP & {7{n1}});\n  wire sub_sel = eE0[6] | (eE0[5:0] == 6'd0);\n\n  // ---- barrel shift amount d = s-1 (input-only + n1) ----\n  wire [4:0] dsub  = 5'd25 - exps[4:0];\n  wire [4:0] dnorm = 5'd9 + {1'b0, n1} - {1'b0, lz[3:0]};\n  wire [4:0] d     = sub_sel ? dsub : dnorm;\n\n  // ---- sticky-accumulating right barrel: W = T >> d ----\n  wire [21:0] v1 = d[4] ? {16'd0, T[21:16]} : T;\n  wire        g1 = d[4] & |T[15:0];\n  wire [18:0] v2 = d[3] ? {5'd0, v1[21:8]} : v1[18:0];\n  wire        g2 = g1 | (d[3] & |v1[7:0]);\n  wire [14:0] v3 = d[2] ? {4'd0, v2[18:4]} : v2[14:0];\n  wire        g3 = g2 | (d[2] & |v2[3:0]);\n  wire [12:0] v4 = d[1] ? {2'd0, v3[14:2]} : v3[12:0];\n  wire        g4 = g3 | (d[1] & |v3[1:0]);\n  wire [11:0] v5 = d[0] ? {1'd0, v4[12:1]} : v4[11:0];\n  wire        g5 = g4 | (d[0] & v4[0]);\n\n  wire [10:0] F    = v5[11:1];\n  wire        guard = v5[0];\n  wire        sticky = g5;\n\n  // ---- round to nearest, ties to even (trail-XOR increment) ----\n  wire [9:0] x = F[9:0];\n  wire [9:0] r1, r2, r3;\n  generate\n    for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : fr0\n      assign r1[i] = (i == 0) ? 1'b1 : x[i-1];\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : fr1\n      if ((i % 2) >= 1) begin : fb1\n        localparam integer k1 = (i & ~1);\n        assign r2[i] = r1[i] & r1[k1];\n      end else begin : fp1\n        assign r2[i] = r1[i];\n      end\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : fr2\n      if ((i % 4) >= 2) begin : fb2\n        localparam integer k2 = (i & ~3) + 1;\n        assign r3[i] = r2[i] & r2[k2];\n      end else begin : fp2\n        assign r3[i] = r2[i];\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire [9:0] trF;\n  wire       trF10;\n  generate\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : eu0\n      assign u1[i] = (i == 0) ? 1'b1 : eE0[i-1];\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : eu1\n      if ((i % 2) >= 1) begin : eb1\n        localparam integer m1 = (i & ~1);\n        assign u2[i] = u1[i] & u1[m1];\n      end else begin : ep1\n        assign u2[i] = u1[i];\n      end\n    end\n    for (i = 0;   -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Unified data path: the result significand is always the 11-bit window\n// T[s+10 : s] of the raw product (guard = T[s-1], sticky = |T[s-2:0]).\n//   normal case : s = 10 - lz + n1   (lz = subnormal normalisation shift)\n//   subnormal   : s = 26 - exps      (subnormal grid 2^-24)\n// The barrel runs on V = 2T with shift d = s (always >= 0) and accumulates\n// the sticky inside the shifter.   -> create_file(content="// fp_mul_e5f10 -- IEEE binary16 multiplier (1+5+10) with subnormal support.\n// Behavioural multiply, then two parallel windows of the product:\n//  - normal  : F = (T >> (9-lz))[12:0]  (13-bit truncated barrel) with the\n//              lz>=10 case muxed in; guard/sticky derived from F and a mask\n//  - subnorm : w = (T >> (26-exps))[10:0] (truncated barrel) with a 12-bit\n//              mask sticky\n// All increments use trail-XOR prefix forms (no ripple adder on the late path).\n\nmodule fp_mul_e5f10 (a, b, y);\n  input  [15:0] a;\n  input  [15:0] b;\n  output [15:0] y;\n\n  wire        sa = a[15];\n  wire        sb = b[15];\n  wire [4:0]  ea = a[14:10];\n  wire [4:0]  eb = b[14:10];\n  wire [9:0]  fa = a[9:0];\n  wire [9:0]  fb = b[9:0];\n\n  wire ea0 = (ea == 5'd0);\n  wire eb0 = (eb == 5'd0);\n  wire eaX = (ea == 5'd31);\n  wire ebX = (eb == 5'd31);\n  wire faz = (fa == 10'd0);\n  wire fbz = (fb == 10'd0);\n  wire za  = ea0 & faz;\n  wire zb  = eb0 & fbz;\n  wire na  = eaX & ~faz;\n  wire nb  = ebX & ~fbz;\n  wire ia  = eaX & faz;\n  wire ib  = ebX & fbz;\n\n  wire nan_in  = na | nb | (ia & zb) | (za & ib);\n  wire inf_in  = ia | ib;\n  wire zero_in = za | zb;\n  wire sgn     = sa ^ sb;\n\n  wire [3:0] clza = fa[9] ? 4'd0 : fa[8] ? 4'd1 : fa[7] ? 4'd2 : fa[6] ? 4'd3 :\n                    fa[5] ? 4'd4 : fa[4] ? 4'd5 : fa[3] ? 4'd6 : fa[2] ? 4'd7 :\n                    fa[1] ? 4'd8 : 4'd9;\n  wire [3:0] clzb = fb[9] ? 4'd0 : fb[8] ? 4'd1 : fb[7] ? 4'd2 : fb[6] ? 4'd3 :\n                    fb[5] ? 4'd4 : fb[4] ? 4'd5 : fb[3] ? 4'd6 : fb[2] ? 4'd7 :\n                    fb[1] ? 4'd8 : 4'd9;\n\n  wire [4:0] lz  = (ea0 ? (5'd1 + {1'b0, clzb}) : 5'd0);\n  wire [3:0] lz4 = lz[3:0];\n  wire       lzge10 = (lz >= 5'd10);\n\n  wire [10:0] ma = {~ea0, fa};\n  wire [10:0] mb = {~eb0, fb};\n\n  wire [21:0] T = ma * mb;\n\n  // ---- exponent arithmetic (input only) ----\n  wire [5:0] exps = {1'b0, ea0 ? 5'd1 : ea} + {1'b0, eb0 ? 5'd1 : eb};\n  wire [6:0] P    = {1'b0, exps} - 7'd15 - {2'b00, lz};\n\n  // trailing ones of P\n  wire [6:0] q1, q2, q3, trP;\n  genvar i;\n  generate\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : pq0\n      assign q1[i] = (i == 0) ? 1'b1 : P[i-1];\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : pq1\n      if ((i % 2) >= 1) begin : b1\n        localparam integer j1 = (i & ~1);\n        assign q2[i] = q1[i] & q1[j1];\n      end else begin : p1\n        assign q2[i] = q1[i];\n      end\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : pq2\n      if ((i % 4) >= 2) begin : b2\n        localparam integer j2 = (i & ~3) + 1;\n        assign q3[i] = q2[i] & q2[j2];\n      end else begin : p2\n        assign q3[i] = q2[i];\n      end\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : pq3\n      if ((i % 8) >= 4) begin : b3\n        localparam integer j3 = (i & ~7) + 3;\n        assign trP[i] = q3[i] & q3[j3];\n      end else begin : p3\n        assign trP[i] = q3[i];\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  // ---- normal result path: F = T[21-lz : 9-lz] ----\n  wire [3:0] g = 4'd9 - lz4;               // window shift (valid for lz <= 9)\n  wire [19:0] n1s = g[3] ? {6'd0, T[21:8]} : T[19:0];\n  wire [15:0] n2s = g[2] ? n1s[19:4] : n1s[15:0];\n  wire [13:0] n3s = g[1] ? n2s[15:2] : n2s[13:0];\n  wire [12:0] n4s = g[0] ? n3s[13:1] : n3s[12:0];\n  wire [12:0] F   = lzge10 ? {T[11:0], 1'b0} : n4s;\n\n  wire        n1   = F[12];\n  wire [10:0] mpre = n1 ? F[12:2] : F[11:1];\n  wire        grdn = n1 ? F[1]    : F[0];\n  wire [8:0]  maskn = lzge10 ? 9'd0 : ((9'd1 << g) - 9'd1);\n  wire        stkn  = (|(T[8:0] & maskn)) | (F[0] & n1);\n  wire        rndn  = grdn & (stkn | mpre[0]);\n\n  wire [9:0] a1, a2, a3, trM;\n  generate\n    for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : fm0\n      assign a1[i] = (i == 0) ? 1'b1 : mpre[i-1];\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : fm1\n      if ((i % 2) >= 1) begin : c1b\n        localparam integer k1 = (i & ~1);\n        assign a2[i] = a1[i] & a1[k1];\n      end else begin : c1p\n        assign a2[i] = a1[i];\n      end\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : fm2\n      if ((i % 4) >= 2) begin : c2b\n        localparam integer k2 = (i & ~3) + 1;\n        assign a3[i] = a2[i] & a2[k2];\n      end else begin : c2p\n        assign a3[i] = a2[i];\n      end\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : fm3\n      if ((i % 8) >= 4) begin : c3b\n        localparam integer k3 = (i & ~7) + 3;\n        assign trM[i] = a3[i] & a3[k3];\n      end else begin : c3p\n        assign trM[i] = a3[i];\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n  wire [9:0] trMx;\n  generate\n    for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : fm5\n      if (i >= 8) begin : c5b\n        assign trMx[i] = trM[i] & trM[7];\n      end else begin : c5p\n        assign trMx[i] = trM[i];\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  wire [9:0] fracn = mpre[9:0] ^ (trMx & {10{rndn}});\n  wire       carry = rndn & &mpre[9:0];\n\n  wire [6:0] A0 = P ^ (trP & {7{n1}});\n  wire       sub_res = A0[6] | ~|A0[5:0];\n\n  wire [6:0] b1w, b2w, b3w, trA;\n  generate\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : fa0\n      assign b1w[i] = (i == 0) ? 1'b1 : A0[i-1];\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : fa1\n      if ((i % 2) >= 1) begin : d1b\n        localparam integer k1 = (i & ~1);\n        assign b2w[i] = b1w[i] & b1w[k1];\n      end else begin : d1p\n        assign b2w[i] = b1w[i];\n      end\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : fa2\n      if ((i % 4) >= 2) begin : d2b\n        localparam integer k2 = (i & ~3) + 1;\n        assign b3w[i] = b2w[i] & b2w[k2];\n      end else begin : d2p\n        assign b3w[i] = b2w[i];\n      end\n    end\n    for (i = 0; i < 7; i = i + 1) begin : fa3\n      if ((i % 8) >= 4) begin : d3b\n        localparam integer k3 = (i & ~7) + 3;\n        assign trA[i] = b3w[i] & b3w[k3];\n      end else begin : d3p\n        assign trA[i] = b3w[i];\n      end\n    end\n  endgenerate\n\n  wire [6:0] eE = A0 ^ (trA & {7{carry}});\n  wire       ovr = ~sub_res & (A0[5] | (&A0[4:0]) |\n                               ((A0[5:0] == 6'd30) & carry));\n\n  // ---- subnormal result path: w = (T >> h)[10:0], h = 25 - exps ----\n  wire [4:0]  h  = 5'd25 - exps[4:0];\n  wire [21:0] u1 = h[4] ? {16'd0, T[21:16]} : T;\n  wire [17:0] u2 = h[3] ? {4'd0, u1[21:8]}  : u1[17:0];\n  wire [13:0] u3 = h[2] ? u2[17:4]          : u2[13:0];\n  wire [11:0] u4 = h[1] ? u3[13:2]          : u3[11:0];\n  wire [10:0] w  = h[0] ? u4[11:1]          : u4[10:0];\n\n  wire [9:0]  f10  = w[10:1];\n  wire        grds = w[0];\n  wire [4:0]  kk   = 5'd15 - exps[4:0];         // h - 10\n  wire [11:0] m12  = ~(12'hFFF << kk[3:0]) & 12'hFFF;\n  wire        stks = |T[9:0] | |(T[21:10] & m12);\n  wire        rnds = grds & (stks | f10[0]);\n\n  wire [9:0] e1w, e2w, e3w, trF;\n  generate\n    for (i = 0; i < 10; i = i + 1) begin : fs0\n      assign e1w[i] = (i == 0) ? 5'd31 :\n                  is_zero ? 5'd0 :\n                  is_sub  ? {4'd0, carry_s} : eE[4:0];\n  wire [9:0] fy = is_nan ? 10'h200 :\n                  (is_inf | is_zero) ? 10'd0 :\n                  is_sub ? fracs : fracn;\n  wire sy = is_nan ? 1'b0 : sgn;\n\n  assign y = {sy, ey, fy};\nendmodule\n", filename='designP.v')

## Summary

**Final best design: `designO.v`** — PASS, 109,374 area_delay_product (area 105.5, delay 1036.9 ps), vs. the starting point's 145,429 (area 108.7, delay 1338 ps) → **~25% cost reduction**.

### 1. Approaches tried (chronological, with results)

| File | Approach | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `design.v` | Starting point (behavioral, barrel on product) | 145,429 baseline |
| `designA/C` | Pre-normalize subnormal significand before multiply | 151,754 / 148,338 — *slower* (serial shifter before multiplier) |
| `designD` | Wallace tree + Kogge-Stone, pre-norm | 140,301 — prefix adder too wide/slow |
| `designF` | **Raw-significand multiply, all shifts after the product, mux-based rounding** | 129,673 — big win |
| `designI` | + input-only sticky masks, fix off-by-one in mask | 117,801 |
| `designN` | + trail-XOR (prefix-AND) increments instead of ripple adders | 111,601 |
| `designO` | Replaced my hand Wallace tree with plain `ma*mb` | **109,374** ← best |
| `designG/H/K/L/M/P` | Various unifications/single-barrel schemes | All failed or regressed |

### 2. What had the most impact
- **Moving all shifters after the multiplier** (`designF`): the multiplier starts directly on the raw significands, so the leading-zero count is computed in parallel instead of serially before it. Saved ~10% delay.
- **Making every shifter select input-only** (lz from the fractions, `25-exps` from the exponents) so no product bit ever drives a shift select — Yosys could then balance the tree freely.
- **Sticky bits via input-only masks** (designI): `sticky = |(T & mask)` where mask derives only from inputs; avoids a second shifter and a deep shifted OR tree. This was worth ~9%.
- **Trail-XOR incrementers** (designN): increment-by-carry computed as `x ^ (trailing_ones(x) & c)` with a prefix-AND tree, applied to rounding and to `exp = P + n1 + carry`. Removed the last ripple adders from the late path (~6%).
- **Plain `ma*mb` beat my hand-written Wallace+Sklansky**: ABC's technology mapping was better than my explicit CSA/prefix structure (109.4k vs 111.6k, and smaller). Don't hand-build what the synthesizer maps well.

### 3. What didn't work
- **Pre-normalizing the subnormal input** (designA): puts a shifter and its LZ logic *before* the multiply, serializing the critical path. Worse despite the smaller post-multiply normalize.
- **Unifying normal+subnormal into one barrel shifter** (designK/L/M): theoretically elegant (the fraction is always a window `T[s+9:s]`, and the two shift formulas coincide on the boundary), but the boundary/window-mux bookkeeping produced repeated bugs (off-by-one in `s`, wrong guard, lz≥10 wraparound). I burned ~8 steps on this without converging.
- **Kogge-Stone prefix adder**: more cells and no faster than what Yosys inferred for `+`.
- **Narrowing the sticky mask to 12 bits** (designP): subtle wraparound/aliasing when the shift was negative or crossed the low half — never got it right; the full-width `~(22'h3FFFFF << h)` mask was correct and cheap enough.
- `target_delay` sweeps: 500 (default) was best; 2000 gave *higher* cost (1516 ps, area barely dropped), 250 identical to 500.

### 4. Lessons learned
- **Verify each arithmetic identity numerically before writing RTL.** Most failures were off-by-ones in shift-window definitions (`guard = T[s-1]`, not `T[s]`; `carry = &x`, not `tr[9]`). A 30-second mental check on two cases (lz=0 and lz=10) would have caught most of them.
- **One bug per change.** When I batched "fix + simplify" edits, I couldn't tell which broke. Steps 51–53 (a single wrong sticky simplification) cost a full evaluation round-trip each.
- **Keep the last known-good file untouched** and copy for experiments — I reverted `designO.v` successfully only because `designN.v` was still intact.
- **Don't hand-infer arithmetic the synthesizer handles**: writing `T = ma*mb` was both smaller and faster than my explicit Wallace/Sklansky version; my effort was better spent on the *surrounding* datapath (shift placement, sticky generation, rounding carry chain), which is where the real structural wins were.
- Correctness-first ordering worked well: the baseline passed immediately, so every subsequent failure was attributable to my own edits rather than a spec misunderstanding.
